# Known port issues

Places where OUR code is known or suspected to differ from retail, found while doing something
else and deliberately NOT fixed on the spot. (Behaviours that are faithful to retail and look like
bugs are in `retail_bugs.md`; this file is the opposite list.)

Rules for an entry: what the code does today, what retail is believed to do, the evidence and how
strong it is, and what a fix must do. A fix is its own commit titled `behaviour:`, proven from
retail's disassembly and gated with resave x5. Status is one of `SUSPECTED` (not yet read in
retail), `CONFIRMED` (retail read, addresses given), `FIXED` (commit).

## 1. `CitizenComp_helper_fe40`: the age-difference `abs()` does nothing — SUSPECTED

**Where.** `src/game/Component/citizencomponent.cpp`, `CitizenComp_helper_fe40` (retail
`0x14010fe40`): finds a partner citizen whose age is inside the description's window
(`+0x48` minimum age, `+0x4c` maximum age difference).

**What the code does.** It takes `abs(candidateAge - ownAge)` by AND-ing the float's bits with a
mask read from the `.rdata` cell `0x1401722b0`. That cell is registered as the 8-byte DOUBLE fabs
mask `0x7fffffffffffffff` (`standalone/reg_component_nature.cpp`, now
`kRdata_1401722b0` in `src/game/Game/exe_constants.h`). The body reads it as a `uint32_t`, i.e. its
low dword `0xffffffff`, so the AND clears nothing and the sign survives: a candidate YOUNGER than
the citizen has a negative difference, which always passes `adiff <= maxDifference`.

**Effect, if retail takes a real `abs`.** The age window is one-sided: any candidate older than the
minimum age and younger than the citizen is accepted however large the gap.

**Evidence.** Found 2026-09-28 by the registry teardown's G-CompA block when the reader of the cell
became the named constant; the block kept the exact value (`static_cast<uint32_t>`), so behaviour
is unchanged by that conversion. NOT yet read in retail. Two explanations are open and the fix
differs: (a) retail's instruction is a float `ANDPS` against a DIFFERENT cell (a 4-byte
`0x7fffffff` mask) and the port resolves the wrong address; (b) retail's cell at `0x1401722b0`
holds a value whose low dword is `0x7fffffff` and the REGISTERED value is wrong — in which case
every other reader of `kRdata_1401722b0` must be re-checked too.

**What a fix must do.** Read `0x14010fe40` in Ghidra (the AND's operand address and width) and the
image bytes at that address; then either point the body at the right constant or correct the
constant. Check the other readers of `0x1401722b0` in the same commit.

## 2. `OrchardRequestState` passes an ADDRESS where the state machine takes the time by value — SUSPECTED

**Where.** `src/game/Component/orchardcomponent_states.cpp`, the call of `StateMachine_TickReimpl`
(retail `0x1400a9660`).

**What the code does.** The old door typedef took a pointer and the caller passed `&timeBuf`; the
body takes the time BY VALUE (`int64_t`). The direct call keeps today's bits
(`reinterpret_cast<int64_t>(timeBuf)`-style cast of the address). Retail is reported to pass the
value 0.

**Evidence.** Found 2026-09-28 by G-CompC; retail's call site not yet quoted with an address.
The trade state machine had the same shape (`tradecomponent_states.cpp`) and now passes the value;
on that path the argument is reported unread.

**What a fix must do.** Read retail's call site, pass what retail passes.

## 3. Growth sub-resources never spawn — FIXED 2026-10-09 (B8 DEC, `ed149bf42`)

**Where.** `src/game/Component/growthcomponent.cpp`, `SpawnSubResource`.

**What the code does.** The pointer to retail `0x14009f5d0` (`CharacterComponent_impl_f5d0`) was
never filled, so the "not placed" branch has always run and sub-resources never spawn. The
teardown wrote that branch out as a `PORT GAP` null arm. A port of the body exists
(`PlayerPath_CharComp_f5d0`) but has never been called from here.

**What a fix must do.** Wire the body in as its own `behaviour:` commit, after checking its
signature against retail's call site, and watch the entity census on the new-game and tutorial
routes.

**USER DECISION 2026-10-09 (B7 close): WIRE IT** -- the second `behaviour:` item of the next step
(NEXT_SESSION §2), the signature checked against 0x14011fba0's call, the census watched on new-game /
tutorial / stress.

**FIXED (B8 DEC, `ed149bf42`):** the call is `PlayerPath_CharComp_f5d0(map, x, y, 4, 0.98f, 1000.0f, 0.0f,
0, 0, nullptr)` as retail's 0x14011fc99..0x14011fce6 (RCX map, EDX x, R8D y, R9D 4, the three floats, two
bytes and the null description on the stack). FOUND in the same read: the spawn-chance test divided the
0.98f UV scale (0x140172140) instead of retail's 1.0f (0x14017214c, 0x14011fc0d) -- corrected in the same
commit. Gated (b8_session/GATE_TABLE.md row DEC): routes clean, resave 5/5 unchanged (a loaded save),
sa-stress 139 fps, worldcmp unchanged (the spread needs sim time past the routes' length).
**B8 CLOSE-A (audit Q1):** the port's `!world` return and `spawnChanceDenom == 0` return were tests retail lacks (0x14011fbd5 reads the singleton unguarded; a zero denominator divides to +inf and never skips) -- dropped.

## 4. `BANISHED_ALLOC_STATS` counted no frees — FIXED

Every free bypassed the instrument, so its live-bytes figure only grew. Fixed 2026-09-28 by the
registry teardown's W0-A0 block (`behaviour:` commit `33d73c9a`): the instrument sits on the
allocator's hook and sees `MemFree`. Diagnostic only; the game was never affected.

## 5. Door typedefs that disagree with their body's parameter WIDTH or sign — SUSPECTED (a list)

When the registry teardown turned door calls into direct calls, the compiler exposed doors whose
typedef did not match the body. Each call keeps the bits the door passed (a cast at the call), so
behaviour is unchanged; none has been read against retail's call site yet.

| where | address | mismatch |
|---|---|---|
| `src/game/Action/action_base_move.cpp` | `0x1400a6a30`, `0x1400a68b0`, `0x1400a6b20`, `0x1400a6c10` | the door passed `int` arguments; the bodies take pointer-width parameters (`0x14009c480` was here too: it takes one parameter now, entry 7) |
| `src/game/Action/action_pf_road.cpp` | `0x1400aa810` | the door took a `uint64` bit pattern where the body takes a pointer |
| `src/game/Action/action_pf_region.cpp` | `0x14001dc00` | `param_4` goes through a `void*` cast |
| `src/game/Action/` | `0x14012c560`, `0x140087fb0` | return type `int` against `uint32_t` |
| `src/game/Component/` (G-CompD) | `0x14011e710` | the body returns `uint64_t`; the door read AL (kept: cast to `char`) |

**What a fix must do.** Per row: read retail's call site (argument registers and their width), then
correct the BODY's declaration or the call, as a `behaviour:` commit only if a value changes.

## 7. `Pf_ZoneRefresh_9c480` declares three parameters retail does not have — FIXED (signature only)

**Where.** The body of retail `0x14009c480`, and its callers in `src/game/Work` (commits
`6067e8a1`, `4b9d6b19`), `src/game/Game/gamegrid_serialize.cpp` (`e200bbb6`) and `src/game/Action`.

**What the code does.** The body is declared with four parameters and hands the fourth to the flood
fill (`0x14009c170`). The direct calls pass `(grid, 0, 0, 0)`.

**Retail** (read 2026-09-28 by the registry teardown's G-Game block). The callers `0x14009f29e`,
`0x1400eb303`, `0x1400e532a`, `0x1400e54ea`, `0x1400e556c` load ONLY RCX. `0x14009c480` WRITES EDX
and R8D before it reads them (`0x14009c4bd`, `0x14009c520` / `c524`) and never touches R9;
`0x14009c170` overwrites R9D at `0x14009c26f` and rewrites its home slot (`c365`, `c3e8`) before any
read. So no incoming value of RDX, R8 or R9 is ever read: the three extra parameters are Ghidra's
(it invents arguments from register use), and what the callers pass cannot matter. The gate showed
no diff on any route or resave.

**What a fix must do.** Declare the body with ONE parameter and make its three former parameters
locals; drop the zeros at the callers. No behaviour change. Same check for `0x14009c170`'s list.

**Fixed** 2026-09-28 by the registry teardown's LEFTOVERS block (commit titled `behaviour:`, "the
zone refresh 0x14009c480 takes one parameter, the flood fill 0x14009c170 three"), after
re-reading both functions. `0x14009c480`: EDX is written at `0x14009c4bd` and per cell at
`0x14009c524` (= EDI, the cell index), R8D at `0x14009c520` (= R14W, the region id), R9 never;
those two locals already existed in the body (`uVar2`, `sVar6`), so the "three former
parameters" needed no new local: two were those locals, the third existed only to be passed on.
`0x14009c170`: the entry spills R9D to its home slot, every read of the slot follows a write
(`0x14009c365`, `0x14009c3e8`) and R9D is overwritten at `0x14009c26f`, so Ghidra's `param_4`
is the local `local_res20` (initial value never read, left uninitialised). Declarations:
`Pf_ZoneRefresh_9c480(int*)`, `Pf_FloodFillRegion_9c170(int*, uint32_t, uint16_t)`. All 15
call sites of `0x14009c480` (Action, Component, Game, Work; most passed non-zero values, e.g.
`(grid, out, flags, destCell)`, `(mapGrid, zr_a, zr_b, 1)`, `(mapInfo, mapInfo, 0, 0)`) and the
two of `0x14009c170` lost their trailing arguments; none had side effects. The caller-less
second body `WorkAcc_ZoneRefresh_9c480` (`work_base_accessor.cpp`) got the same list. No value
that is ever read changed.

## 9. Bodies declared `void` whose callers read a return value — FIXED / no reader

| address | body | what the caller reads |
|---|---|---|
| `0x14009ce00` | `RoadTool_sub_ce00` (road A*) | the path length retail leaves in EAX (`roadtool.cpp`, `roadtool_calcpath.cpp`). The readers were LEFT as resolves by the registry teardown because the signature cannot be settled without retail |
| `0x1400fd960` | — | a return value |
| — | `Pf_RoadMeshRebuild_f370` | a return value |

Today the callers read whatever the body's last computation left in EAX. **What a fix must do:**
read each function's epilogue in retail, give the body its return type and value.

**2026-09-28, the registry teardown's LEFTOVERS block:**
- `0x14009ce00` — FIXED (commit titled `behaviour:`, "the road A* 0x14009ce00 returns the path
  length"). Retail ends in a tail `JMP 0x1400a6e30` at `0x14009cf41` (only callee-saved
  registers restored before it); `0x1400a6e30` takes EAX from its `CALL 0x1400a6d50` at
  `0x1400a6edf` and leaves it to the `RET` at `0x1400a6ef8`. So the value IS computed by the
  body: our `RoadTool_helper_6e30` already returned it as `int`. `RoadTool_sub_ce00` now
  returns `int` (`return RoadTool_helper_6e30(...)`), and the five readers in `roadtool.cpp` /
  `roadtool_calcpath.cpp` call it by name. Its 5th parameter is never read (retail overwrites
  its stack slot at `0x14009cf22`). `standalone/reg_keystone_a_zone.cpp` still has a local
  `void` prototype of it (return types are not mangled; W2 deletes the row).
- `0x1400fd960` (`PlacementToolPlace_FlipVariant_d960`) and `0x1400af370`
  (`Pf_RoadMeshRebuild_f370`) — no reader reads a value any more: their callers
  (`placementtool.cpp`, `tool_base_tile.cpp`, `keystone_a_removalcalcpath.cpp`) call them by
  name as `void`, and the only "value" the old doors read was the upper bytes of RAX under a
  `bool` slot whose contract is AL. Nothing to fix unless a caller is found that reads more.

## 10. `0x140172248` is 1000.0f; the code around it says otherwise — FIXED (B6 UI, row 70, a13db9d97)

The image bytes at `0x140172248` are `00 00 7a 44` (1000.0f, read in Ghidra 2026-09-28). A comment
in the placement code calls the cell 0.5, and `src/game/Player/tool_base_tile*.cpp` hard-codes
`250.0f` where retail reads this cell. **What a fix must do:** read retail's instruction at the
`tool_base_tile` site and use `kRdata_140172248` if that is the operand.

## 11. `String::ToInteger` and a `memmove` were never reachable from the UI edit widgets — FIXED, not exercised

`src/engine/ui`: `NumberEditElement::FinalizeText`, `SpinnerElement` (3 sites) and `DocumentText`
resolved `0x18003f620` (`System::String::ToInteger`), and `EditElement` resolved `0x18008a290`
(a `memmove`), by ADDRESS. Nothing registered either address, so both resolved to null and the first
call would have jumped to 0. Fixed 2026-09-28 (`behaviour:` commit `638c4b3a`, the registry
teardown's RT-UI block): direct calls of `System::String::ToInteger` and `std::memmove`.
No gate route types into a number field or edits text, so the fix is compiled and unit-built but
has NOT been seen running. A route that types into the New Game seed field would exercise it.

## 12. `AttendanceComponent_GetJobHostComponent` was `void`; `FindAvailableBuilding` read its return — FIXED

**Where.** `src/game/Component/attendancecomponent.cpp` (retail `0x140002e10`, a by-value
`InternalBasePtr` return: RAX is the return buffer), read by `FindAvailableBuilding`
(`src/game/Game/town_workplace_registry.cpp`, retail `0x14008d100`).

**What happened.** The body was declared `void`. The reader called it through a function pointer
typed `void* (*)(…)` and dereferenced RAX. Through the registry's pointer RAX happened to hold the
buffer; when the registry teardown made it a direct call, the -O2 build left `1` in RAX and the
stress town crashed (`READ of 0000000000000001`, `town_workplace_registry.cpp:286`, reached from
`CitizenEmploy_SeatWorkers`). No gate route seats workers through this path; only `sa-stress` does.

**Fix.** The body returns `outPtr`; the reader calls it by name. Entry 9 lists the same class of
defect. **The lesson for B.4:** a cast of a function to another signature is never "keeping today's
bits" — what RAX holds after a `void` function is not defined, and changes with the call form.

## 13. `TradeWork::Validate` walks a stale entry of the post's citizen tree — SETTLED (B6 TRADE: the mirrored find-remove core, row 742, fixed by a4ba1f7ed; found 2026-09-29)

**Symptom.** The stress town with the virtual merchant customer
(`BANISHED_AUTOSTART=1 BANISHED_STRESS=1 BANISHED_TRADE_SELL=600 BANISHED_TRADE_SELL_SEED=50`, debug
exe) dies with exit 3 after 17-19 trades (frame ~10,800-12,000): `Object::Base::IsA`
(`TypeInfo.h:338`) <- `WorkBase_ValidateTask_f7100` (`work_base_reimpl.cpp`) <-
`TradeWork::Validate()` (`tradework.cpp:545`) <- the citizen AI's idle helpers
(`citizenai_housing_idle.cpp`) <- `CitizenAIComponent::State_Idle`. The object whose `Type()` is
read is garbage (`READ of 0x18900000073` at B.4's tip; `EXEC of 0x6000000060` at the base: the
vtable slot itself was garbage there).

**Not a B.4 regression.** Reproduced on `b2a5434c` (the step's base) with the same route: same three
frames. No block of B.4 changed `tradework.cpp` or the validate body.

**What is known.** `TradeWork::Validate` walks the in-order BST of entities assigned to the trading
post and validates each; the last log line before the fault in the B.4 run is a citizen dying
(`[Burial] NO GRAVE … cause=0(starvation)`). A citizen that leaves the world is still an entry of
the post's tree. Retail has no check at the fault site (`IsA` on the entry): do NOT add one. The
question for the fix is which retail path removes a dying / leaving citizen from the work's tree
(the citizen's `OnRemove` 0x140113ca0 and the workplace release 0x140116540 / 0x140115a50 are the
candidates) and whether the tree's port of it reaches TradeWork. The route is not part of any gate.

WORK-c, 2026-09-29: the removal path, read against retail. Retail removes a citizen from the
post's tree only through the citizen's action stack: `CharacterComponent::OnRemove` 0x140109980
clears the stack through 0x1400c5400, which calls slot 8 of every chained action; the
`CompleteTask` that `TradeWork::Execute` queued (through 0x1400bf790, its +0x20 = the citizen,
+0x30 = the work id `Work+0x20`) answers with `CheckEntity` 0x1400c5ac0 -> 0x1400f8800, which
finds the work in the scheduler's id map (`WorkScheduler::trees[0]`, +0x30) and calls its slot 7,
`TradeWork::OnCancel` 0x1400f5d10 = 0x14002c690 find-and-remove keyed on the citizen. Slot 6
(0x1400f5d20, the CompleteTask's own completion through 0x1400f8760) removes it the same way.
The tree's port of each link was read and matches retail: `TradeWork::Validate` (0x1400f5ae0),
`Work::ValidateTask` (0x1400f7100), `CharacterComponent::OnRemove`, the stack clear (it re-reads
the array and its size every iteration, as retail), `ActCt_TaskReassign_8800`, `FindTaskNode`
(the same +0x30 tree the scheduler posts into), `TradeWork::OnCancel` / `OnComplete`, and the
chain `Execute` builds (twoWay = 1: the CompleteTask follows the pickup). The ONE difference on
the path: the tree's `CompleteTask::CheckEntity` (`completetask.cpp`) returns early when
`GameUpdateLoop::_singleton` or its +0x118 scheduler is null; retail 0x1400c5ac0 has no such test
(14d). It cannot drop a removal while a town is running. NOT FOUND: where the stale entry comes
from. Two open questions for the fix, neither read: whether the tree's death path (the
`[Burial]` handler in `citizen_lifeevent_handler.cpp` and what destroys the citizen entity after
it) reaches `CharacterComponent::OnRemove` at all, and whether a citizen can hold a post
assignment with no CompleteTask left on its stack (a builder that pushes without clearing first,
e.g. 0x1400c0ad0 with its cancel flag 0, runs on top of the old chain). SUSPECTED, not CONFIRMED.
No `behaviour:` commit; no check added.

B6 TRADE, 2026-10-09: found by bisect and already fixed on main. On 0518766d0, the commit
that filed this row, the route's debug exe hangs at shutdown on the default budget (exit 4,
frame 12,953). The main thread spins in `TradeWork::Cleanup` (0x1400f6be0) under
`EntityList_Shutdown`: it clears the leftmost citizen's stack and the CompleteTask cancel
never takes the citizen out of the set. That is the same unremoved entry the Validate fault
walks once the citizen is freed. A first-parent bisect of 0518766d0..338120596 on that signal
names merge dfea96f41, commit a4ba1f7ed (row 742). The shared find-remove core 0x14002c690
under `TradeWork::OnComplete` / `OnCancel` walked mirrored (right on key < node), so a removal
found its node only at the root or on the mirrored path. Restoring the mirrored walk on
338120596 brings it back: 52 of 70 slot 6 / 7 removals missed and the same Cleanup hang
returned (exit 4, frame 12,527). On 338120596 the route is clean on both exes with
`BANISHED_STRESS_FRAMES=40000`: perf exit 0, 65 trades, frame 94,299; debug exit 0,
65 trades, frame 83,828. Every insert met a removal that found its node (135/135 and
143/143). Both open questions above are answered: the death path does reach
`CharacterComponent::OnRemove` (`MarkEntityForRemoval`, entity flags 0xcf, bit 1 set), and
a citizen holding an entry with no CompleteTask is pruned by Validate while it lives. The
one retail difference on the path, `CompleteTask::CheckEntity`'s null singleton / scheduler
return, is dropped in 90d256918 (0x1400c5ac0 has none). The 14d KEPT (WORK-c) bullet naming
it is settled by that commit. `AbstractImpl`'s twin guard (14d KEPT ACT-b) is left to block D.
No trader died while still in the set on any run, so the death-to-OnCancel link ran only as
code read. | FIXED (B6 TRADE, 90d256918): the stale entry was row 742's mirrored 0x14002c690
walk (fixed in a4ba1f7ed); the CheckEntity guard retail lacks is dropped.

## 14. Differences from retail found by the B.4 blocks and left — a list (2026-09-29)

Each was found by a B.4 block while it converted the body, read against retail by that block, and
deliberately NOT changed inside the conversion. Status `CONFIRMED` means the block quoted retail's
address; `SUSPECTED` means it did not read retail. Every fix is its own `behaviour:` commit.
The block's hand-in is summarised in `docs/history/endgame_b/b4_session/GATE_TABLE.md`; the site
in the code carries a comment.

The Linux bed stays lavapipe (CPU); `tools/linux_run.sh` pins lavapipe's manifest (`VK_ICD_FILENAMES` / `VK_DRIVER_FILES`) so the loader never offers dzn, and keeps the `LINUX_VK_ICD` override (`system` = the loader's own choice, for a real GPU). GPU-speed Linux testing needs a native Linux boot (the P5 real-GPU checklist in `src/render/vulkan/README.md`) |

### 14d. Guards the tree has and retail lacks — and guards B.4 dropped

Retail's unguarded derefs are load-bearing (CLAUDE.md). The first group is kept and listed; the
second group was removed when a typed member replaced a guarded helper, and is safe only under the
stated condition.

While a track is not flushed the resampler holds back RESAMPLER_MAX_PADDING_FRAMES (7 with SSE) of input as the filter's right-hand context; from the second pull on that hold-back is inside "available" and so inside the request, but a fresh stream has nothing queued and its first request leaves it out. Handing over exactly `additional` converted 7-8 frames fewer than the device period and the device filled the end of that first period with silence (SDL_audio.c: `br < buffer_size` -> memset, or in the mixing path a missing contribution): a 0.15 ms notch 21 ms into every one-shot and every ambience start (the device runs at its own rate, and the pitch ratio resamples the rest). The first pull now hands over the padding too; the surplus shrinks the next request. NOT the reported END click: the END path was checked and is clean (the last pass hands over exactly the remaining bytes, the device silences the rest, OnStreamEnd one pass later; `tests/unit/audio_feed_test.cpp` pins it, and the harness shows every one-shot's frame count equal to the expected), the decoded tails of all 56 shipped SoundBuffers end within 36 S16 steps of zero (`tools/audio_tail/audio_tail.cpp` over `tools/audio_tail/pkg_sounds.py`'s extraction; the largest final sample is Rain's 36, Cow3's -17, Saw0's -27), and no shipped sound is a bird one-shot (the birds are in the looping Field / Forest / Wetland ambience, `loopForever` = 1 in the resource). ### 14e. Stores that may normalise where retail copies

- `optionsdialog.cpp:745,748`: `inputCfg->_mouseClipping = checkMouseClip->_checked != 0;` and
  `_mouseDisplay` likewise. UI's `EnableCursor` tests `Input::Configuration::_mouseDisplay` as a
  `bool` where retail compares a byte. SETTLED by R-ENG from retail: the three Input::Configuration
  flags ARE `bool` -- `Input::Configuration::Startup` reads them through
  `?Get@Persistence@IO@@QEBA_NPEBD0AEA_N@Z` (0x1800b8290, `AEA_N` = `bool&`), and `EnableCursor`
  0x180122580 tests `CMP byte ptr [RAX+0xa],0` / `CMOVNZ` (0x180122599), a bool's zero test.
  `ApplyInputSettings` 0x140072360 copies the check box's +0xd8 byte RAW: `MOVZX EAX,byte [R8+0xd8]`
  / `MOV byte [RDX+0x8],AL` (0x140072390 / 0x14007239f) and `MOVZX EAX,byte [RDX+0xd8]` /
  `MOV byte [RCX+0xa],AL` (0x1400723b9 / 0x1400723c2) -- a bool-to-bool copy in the source, so
  `CheckElement::_checked` (+0xd8, `uint8_t` in the tree) is the retail `bool`. The tree's `!= 0` is
  value-identical while `_checked` holds 0 / 1; the faithful spelling is the raw copy
  `inputCfg->_mouseClipping = checkMouseClip->_checked;` (DLG's file, not changed). CONFIRMED. Several flags became `bool` in R-UI-b and R-VIDEO
  (`SpriteSheet::m_field50`, `TextDescription::_b84/_b85`, the ribbon / scroll `_vertical`,
  RenderState's six flags). Identical only if the byte is always 0 or 1. SUSPECTED; the settings
  flags were the same question and retail copies raw (0x14007248f), so those are `uint8_t`.
- (ACT-b) Now `bool`: `AnimateNaturalResource` +0x2C (0x1400c5660 serializes it as bool),
  `ConsumeRawMaterial` +0x38, `CreateRawMaterial` +0x50 / +0x51, `DropOffRawMaterial` +0x20,
  `HuntAnimal` +0x38, `PerformWork::useTool` +0x40, `PickupStoredRawMaterial` +0x40 and
  `RemoveNaturalResource` +0x30. The typed ctors copy the byte (B11: `movzbl` / `mov %dl`, as the
  old ctors did); the NORMALISATION (`setne`) is at the seven `uint8_t` -> `bool` conversions of
  the C entry points, where retail's ctor copies the byte raw:
  `animatenaturalresource.cpp:145` (0x1400c57d0), `removenaturalresource.cpp:218` (0x1400cadf0),
  `dropoffrawmaterial.cpp:276` (0x1400c7cd0), `consumerawmaterial.cpp:392` (0x1400c63b0, raw copy
  read at 0x1400c640f), `pickupstoredrawmaterial.cpp:421` (0x1400ca520),
  `createrawmaterial.cpp:379` (0x1400c71a0) and `performwork.cpp:252` (the `void*` ctor reached
  through `Pf_PerformWorkCtor7_9720`, 0x1400c9720, raw copy read at 0x1400c9796). Retail not
  re-read for the other five. H8 checked in B11: every caller of the seven passes a literal 0 / 1
  (directly, or through `CitizenAI_PerformWorkCtor4_c1070`'s 1 / 0, `WorkAct_GatherRawMatCtor_bf790`'s
  `useAnimal` / `param_14` 0 / 1, `WorkAct_PerformWorkCtor_c1eb0`'s 0 / 1), so no stored value
  differs from the old tree's. The array appends of ConsumeRawMaterial /
  PickupStoredRawMaterial keep retail's `&array[i] != nullptr` test, which an -O2 compiler may fold. B6 WORK: `performwork.cpp:252` is `useTool = useToolArg` (bool to bool, a byte copy as retail 0x1400c9796): the `void*` entry point is gone, nothing to change there.
- (CAI-5, row 241) `CitizenAIComponent::State_Leisure` (`citizenai_leisure.cpp`) now reads
  `HappinessComponent::registered` (+0x28, `bool` in the header) by name; at -O2 GCC turns
  `if (!(... || !registered)) happinessMode = true;` into a COPY of the byte into `happinessMode`,
  which later becomes PlayAnimation's fifth argument. Retail tests the byte and stores a literal 1
  (0x14010adaa `CMP byte ptr [RDI+0x28],0` / 0x14010adb0 `MOV byte ptr [RBP+0x6f],0x1`); the old
  tree read the qword and tested its low byte. Identical while `registered` holds 0 / 1 (every
  writer in the tree stores a C++ bool). CONFIRMED.
- (P2 CIT-1, row 311) `Cemetery_SpawnTombstone_6a90` (`citizen_lifeevent_handler.cpp`, 0x140046a90)
  ORs the "a walkable cell changed" bit into Map +0xA2 as a BYTE (`|= uint8_t(stateWasWalk)`), while
  `Map.h` declares +0xA2 `bool field_0xA2` (ctor 1). The site stays a byte OR through a cast
  (`// LEFT`); typing it as `bool |=` is value-identical only while the byte holds 0 / 1. Retail's
  instruction not read. SUSPECTED. B6 UI (40063fb93, `Component/citizen_lifeevent_handler.cpp`): retail is `SETNZ AL; OR byte ptr [map+0xa2],AL` (0x140046afd) and `Map::field_0xA2` is `uint8_t` since B.4 H8, so the OR was already retail; the body's standalone guards were dropped (0x140057b20 keeps its own).
- (P2 DLG-1, row 447) `EventNotifyDialog::OnUpdate` (`eventnotifydialog.cpp`) now reads the category filter `EventLog::field_0x10[category]` by name (`bool[6]` in `Game/EventLog.h`, GAME-a's); at -O2 GCC copies that byte into the local `hasNewEvents` where the old tree stored a literal 1 (`MOV R14D,1` -> `MOV R14D,R13D`). Identical while the byte holds 0 / 1: the ctor writes {0,1,1,1,1,1} and `EventLogUI::ToggleNotification` copies `CheckElement::_checked` (0 / 1). Retail not read. SUSPECTED.
- (X-G1-2, row 560) `TownHallUI::ProfessionNode` (`Dialog/TownHallUI.h`) declares +0x28 as ONE `int32_t balance`
  where the shared AVL retrace 0x140013480 (`AvlRebalance`, Action/PathAvl.h) reads and writes an int16 balance at
  +0x28 and an int16 height at +0x2a. townhallui.cpp therefore still hands the tree to the retrace as `AvlNode30`
  (two casts, LEFT); any read of `balance` as an int32 in the dialog sees both halves. CONFIRMED (X-G1-2b): retail's
  0x140013480 reads `MOVZX word ptr [+0x2a]` and writes `MOV word ptr [R8+0x28]` / `[R8+0x2a]`; the same holds for
  `AgricultureNode` +0x20 / +0x22 (0x1400149a0, `TownHall_AvlRebalance28`). FIXED on b4x-G1-2b: both nodes declare
  int16 `balance` / `height` (layout unchanged, asserted), the dialog's node inits write both halves (H7), and the

- (X-G1-2, row 561) `PathCostNode::balance` (`Entity/CitizenBehavior.h`) is likewise one int32 at +0x28 while the
  insert 0x140025a10 (`PathCostTreeInsert`, which runs on `AvlNode30`) and its retrace 0x14000e360 treat +0x28 /
  +0x2a as two int16s; PathGraph's {m_bstRoot, m_bstCount} are two fields, not one Set header, so citizenbehavior.cpp
  keeps two casts per insert (LEFT). CONFIRMED (X-G1-2b): 0x14000e360 reads `MOVZX word ptr [+0x2a]` and writes
  `MOV word ptr [RDX+0x28]` / `[RDX+0x2a]`. FIXED on b4x-G1-2b: `PathCostNode` declares int16 `balance` / `height`
  (layout unchanged, asserted; the tree reads neither, the C allocators write them). The two insert casts stay

### 14f. Names that mislead (naming only)

- CitizenComponent +0x238 is the HOME and +0x248 the WORKPLACE; the bodies the tree and Ghidra call
  `AssignWorkplace` (0x1401163d0) and `AssignHousing` (0x140111ee0) are the other way round.
- `GameUpdateLoop::m_tutorial` holds a `WorkScheduler`, `m_imageCapture` the `Minimap`.
- `SelectDescription::_ref20` (townhallui reads it by that name); `Profession::priority` (+0x50) is
  a carry / capacity amount; `ClearAreaComponent` +0x48 `clearState` is used as a capacity;
  `ToolUI` +0x40 `hasBorderTitle` is the pin flag; CitizenDescription's `min/maxPregnancyAge` are
  the spawn age range in debugui; the list DebugUI's disaster trigger picks from is
  `CitizenBehavior::citizens` (the tree called it the building list).
- Ghidra labels: `0x140112ae0 CitizenComponent_ConsumeFuel` is the happiness update;
  `0x140114900 CitizenComponent_OnCreate` serializes CitizenDescription; `g_PauseDialog_Vtable`
  holds MainMenuDialog's slots and `g_ReloadDialog_Vtable` MessageDialog's; `0x140115de0`
  ("GetHappiness") is the carry count.
- Two tree bodies both claim retail 0x1400049c0 (`Reimpl_ExternalPtrArray_Serialize`,
  `Desc_LoadExternalArray`).
- `Video::Device::gap50[0x10]` (+0x50 / +0x58) is not a gap: MainLoop's ctor stores the video-error
  and video-restart callbacks there (the profile build's `Video::Device::SetVideoErrorFuncs`,
  0x1800fd860, inlined); `engine.cpp` still writes them as raw bytes because the header (R-VIDEO)
  has no named fields.
- `Action::_entity` (+0x10, ACT) is the NEXT action of a chain wherever the action stack is walked
  (0x1400c5400, the inlined appends of 0x1400c1530 / 0x1400bef70, 0x1400c4f10). (WORK-c)
- The Work action-chain builders are not constructors: `WorkAct_*Ctor_*` (0x1400c1eb0, 0x1400c0ad0,
  0x1400c2490, 0x1400bb0a0, 0x1400be6e0, 0x1400ba9f0, 0x1400bffd0, 0x1400bb7b0, 0x1400beae0,
  0x1400bef70) each clear the citizen's stack and push a chain; Ghidra's `ShowTool_Action_Ctor4`
  (0x1400c1530) and `Pathfinding_FindPath` (0x1400bf790) are two more.
  `WorkBase_TaskListCleanup_c5400` is CharacterComponent's action-stack clear, `WorkBase_FindComponent_6270`
  is FindComponent<StorageComponent>, and `Entity_FindConsumeProduce_b6f0`'s old label
  "Entity_FindStateMachine" is wrong. `Work::field_20` is the work id the scheduler keys on,
  `field_28` the target cell. (WORK-c)
- INVENTORY_A's `ToolUI_ChildDialog` is `UI::DialogInstance` (the exe's copy of its table,
  0x14015f5a0); `fn_FindComponent_MeshInstance` finds the StatusIconComponent (cell 0x1401c7e08);
  Ghidra's `Entity_impl_a120` is StatusBarUI's status-icon handler 0x14007a120 and
  `ToolUI_helper_e100` is `UIDescription::AddControllers` 0x14012e100 (DLG-d).
- Work (WORK-b): `Pickup::candidateList` is an `ExternalPtr<Profession>`, not a list; the
  `citizenEntity` locals of `Pickup` slot 5 and the pickup gate hold a Profession; the finder
  forwarders `WorkBase_FindComponent_2f90` ("MeshInstance") and `WorkBase_FindComponent_2ed0`
  ("Position") find the StatusIconComponent and the InteractComponent; `WorkAct_HarvestAreaCtor_c1530`
  is named for its caller. HarvestArea / HarvestOrchard / Herbalist `Cleanup` walks the LEFT child
  (the tree's comments said "rightmost"; corrected in the converted files).

- `Action::_entity` is now `Action::_next` (ACT-a renamed it; the WORK-c row above predates it).
  `TrackerComponent::displayed` (+0x28) and `produced` (+0x38): 0x140058fd0 upserts into +0x28 and
  copies it into +0x38 when the year changes, the opposite of the header's comments. The
  `Pf_EntityResolvePos_2e20` / `Pf_RecursiveChildSum_123070` pair work on the InteractComponent
  node tree (`InteractNodePosition` / `InteractNodeCount`), and `ActCNR_NR_*` on the ModelComponent
  mesh selection (`ModelMeshGroupCount`, `ModelMeshCount`, `ModelSelectMesh`), not on a
  NaturalResourceComponent. (ACT-a)
- (ACT-b) `CreateRawMaterial` +0x28 is the working CitizenComponent, and the yield picks
  `_lowCreateCount` while its `education` < `CitizenDescription::maxEducation` (the tree said
  building age / maturity); `RemoveNaturalResource`'s "growth value / threshold" are the same pair
  and +0x248 is the workplace. `HuntAnimal` +0x20 is the HuntComponent and +0x28 the hunting
  citizen (the tree: "animal", "herd"); 0x140005500 finds the AnimalComponent and 0x1400227e0 the
  WildAnimalComponent (the tree: a model component, "HerdComponent"); 0x1400052c0 is
  NaturalResourceComponent and 0x140003f60 ModelComponent (createnaturalresource.cpp's old banner
  swapped them). `MoveTo`: +0x468 plays when a path starts and +0x46C when it ends (the tree's
  idle / walk were swapped); +0x470 ("bridge flag") is SetMovementCosts' profile; +0x474 ("road
  flag") is set when the nearest other character within 0.5 faces this one; +0x30 ("path data") is
  the CharacterDescription; +0x20 / +0x24 are a PathTicket. `PickupStoredRawMaterial` +0x20 is the
  carrying citizen and +0x28 the storage entity. `ShowTool_ShowCarried`'s "+0xb8 +0x120 sceneId"
  store lands on `CitizenComponent::toolId` (+0x1D8), its "StateMachineComponent" is
  `CitizenComponent::character`, and its scale Vector4 is `MeshInstance::_color`. The function the
  tree called `Game::UpdateTimedEvents` (0x1400a5d10) is `RegionPairCache::Expire`.

| 890 | the forced device-lost route after ONE injection (`BANISHED_FORCE_DEVICE_LOST`, `b4_session/devlost.sh`; S4 coordinator, 2026-10-05) | after the single injected `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_RESET` every later Present reports hr 0x887a0005 and the exe runs a complete device-lost round trip per frame; the working set grows about 100 MB per round trip (20.8 GB at 600 s, 207-219 round trips), which stopped the harness for low system memory in the third B.4 session and again at the S4 close | not compared with retail (retail's window is `reference_device_lost_loader_race`; whether retail's recreate clears the removed state, and what each round trip leaks here, are unread) | DEFERRED (user, 2026-09-27; lifted only by the user) with device-lost. `devlost.sh` ends the run after `DLMAX` (5) round trips. READ at B5 (`b5_session/DEVLOST_FINDING.md`, 2026-10-05): the harness fires ONCE (a `fired` latch); every later 0x887a0005 is a REAL removal of a genuinely recreated device (`D3D11CreateDevice` once per trip), recurring faster each trip; `GetDeviceRemovedReason()` (printed since B5 CTX) reads 0 on the injected frame and 0x887a0005 with no sub-cause on the real trips. Leading hypothesis: the exe-side PORT GAP pair `GameUpdateLoop::OnVideoError / OnVideoRestart` (retail 0x140095970 / 0x140095930, gameupdateloop.cpp:2345-2352) leaves game-owned buffers on the dead device, which the new context binds; the old `ID3D11Device` is never Released on the lost path (retail's own `d3dDevice = nullptr` before Shutdown -- keep) so each trip keeps one more device alive. The ~100 MB per trip is not measurable from the logs. Next: port the GameUpdateLoop pair (`behaviour:` against the exe), then re-measure RE-OPENED (user, 2026-10-11 evening): the deferral lifted; the task is docs/runtime_port/NEXT_SESSION.md section 2.4 **FIXED 2026-10-11 night (merge `8b89fcfd5`, branch d5-devlost, opus agent; hand-in `phase_d/handins/DEVLOST.md`)**: the pair ported as retail 0x140095970 / 0x140095930 -- OnVideoError releases WorldGeometry's four buffer companions + height image (0x1400b0be0) and the minimap's two images (ImageBuffer::Release 0x1800f33c0, newly ported); OnVideoRestart recreates the buffers, rebuilds + re-uploads the terrain mesh, height image and water mesh (0x1400b0c50), then the minimap (0x1400a59a0); the menu loop's twin 0x14009a8d0 / 0x14009a8b0 wired too. Forced route (`runs/DEVLOST1`, inject at Present 12000, DLMAX 5): INJECT=1 COMPLETE=1, ZERO later hr=0x887a0005, working set 296-323 MB flat for 220 s after the trip (was ~100 MB per frame), the run quit itself through the pause menu at 20000; gate 0 problems. The hypothesis held: the non-dynamic terrain / minimap objects crossed devices |

Not long GPU work of ours: an event-query probe on the immediate context reached the GPU in 15-30 ms right after the last good `DeviceData_Resize`, and the reset follows within ~20 ms, at the first `Present` after that resize. The trigger is the window flapping on a first run (no saved window size): the desktop-sized client is framed by `AdjustDisplay` (`(0,0 2560x1440)` -> `(-6,-6 2576x1479)`), Windows clamps or maximizes it, `SizeWindow` re-arms the reset, the next `AdjustDisplay` puts it back; while another window holds the foreground each reset fails at `SetFullscreenState(FALSE)` = `DXGI_ERROR_NOT_CURRENTLY_AVAILABLE`. Once the device is removed, `ResizeBuffers` answers `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED` on every retry, `BeginCommands` never reaches `Present`, so the device-lost recovery (`ContextData::Finish` -> `OnVideoError` / `OnVideoRestart`) never starts: the run spins in the reset (77k failed resizes in 240 s) or crashes at the first read-back (the cursor). Cause of the storm, re-read: an empty settings.cfg boots in EXCLUSIVE FULLSCREEN (retail's default), not at a desktop-sized window; the holder's foreground makes `DeviceData_OnActivate` drop to windowed and back, and while `SetFullscreenState(TRUE)` is refused (no foreground, or the holder owns the output in exclusive fullscreen) `BeginCommands` retries the reset every frame, as retail does. The port's own part: SDL's borderless style keeps `WS_CAPTION | WS_SYSMENU` (hidden by `WM_NCCALCSIZE`), `ResizeTarget` sized the window with that style's frame, and each retry shrank the client by 6 x 29 with a `WM_SIZE` that re-armed the reset (`src/platform/sdl/display_sdl.cpp` `SetFramed` + SDL's `GetWindowStyle`). (a) `src/platform/win/app_win.cpp` `PreSdlInitHints`: `SDL_BORDERLESS_WINDOWED_STYLE=0`, the borderless window is `WS_POPUP` as retail's (commit d3ac81bea). The refused-fullscreen retry itself (hundreds to thousands of resets while another window holds the foreground) is retail's and stays. (b) `src/render/dx11/contextdata.cpp` `BeginCommands`: a reset that fails with `GetDeviceRemovedReason() != 0` runs Finish's device-lost recovery (`RecoverLostDevice`, lifted out of `Finish` unchanged); the `OnVideoError` crash it then exposed is row 1332, a retail bug (`docs/retail_bugs.md` section 10). Both are ONE row, `fix.boot_device_lost` (ruling: the reset-path recovery is behaviour retail lacks, so it sits under the row): seams `Engine::MainLoop::_extFixBootDeviceLost` and `BootDeviceLostBackend::SetFixFlag` (`src/render/common/boot_device_lost/`; commits 5e7fef9ac, f1192d836). Under `/profile:faithful` both halves are off and retail's spin or crash remains, the intended faithful behaviour. Repro route (debug exe, empty settings.cfg, holder ~3 s in with the foreground handed to it, logs `scratchpad\t-1334fix\runs\`): before 5 of 6 crashed (`base_h2_*`: 3 in `OnVideoError`, 2 at the cursor's `Map`); after 10 of 10 exit 0 at the menu (`fix3k_h2_*`; again 10 of 10 with the recovery under the row, `fix4_h2_*` / `fix4b_h2_*`, one through the reset route; 4 recovered through the reset route, every run through at least one Present-route round trip, one run 3 trips); 1 of 10 in an earlier batch waited 60 s without the foreground while the holder owned exclusive fullscreen (retail's retry; with the holder closed the run proceeds). The GPU resets themselves (nvlddmkm 153) come with two games switching exclusive fullscreen on one output and are not the port's. Not done: Win32 `WM_SIZE` is synchronous (a `ResizeTarget` clamp is absorbed inside the reset) and SDL's is queued, so a clamped windowed reset costs one extra reset here |

| 1340 | `CitizenUI::OnUpdateLayout` (`Dialog/citizenui.cpp`), the `textNameIs` name | skipped the `SetParameter(0, name)` on `textNameIs` ("redundant, the primary textName already shows the name"), so the citizen panel's status line, the name followed by the action text, read " is idling." with no name | retail `0x140064ae0` (GOG `Application-x64.exe`), after `buttonHome`'s Enable: `CheckPtr(&citizen); CheckPtr(this + 0x90); TextElement::SetParameter(*(TextElement**)(this + 0x90), 0, (String*)(citizen + 0x28))`, with no type, address-range or parameter-array test; the status line is `textNameIs` + `textAction` (`StringTable.rsc:citizenActions`, "idling.") | FIXED: the same unconditional `SetParameter(0, ai->name)`; `textNameIs` is declared `UI::TextElement*` as `textAction` is, so the cast goes. Seen in a running game on this branch alone, 2026-10-30 (exe `OpenBA_nameis2`): the line reads "<name> is idling."; an earlier form of the set, under the primary name's guards, was seen on another branch on 2026-10-29 |

## macOS Metal port

- **Resolved: retained terrain-buffer copies.** Loading repeatedly cloned the
  full terrain buffer for chunk SetData calls. About 6609 MB of the 6795 MB
  graphics allocation was attributed to those copies. Loading lacked a local
  autorelease pool, extending their lifetime. The fix scopes autoreleases and
  writes in place unless encoded or in-flight draws still use the allocation;
  completion tickets track that use. The recorded normal-game graphics sample
  fell from 6779 to 167 MB, total footprint from 7285 to 668 MB. Separate
  60-second validation runs passed; the earlier 25-minute soak was not repeated
  after the fix. See [measurements](reference/macos_perf.md).
- **Depth precision:** Metal uses D32F+S8 rather than retail D24S8. Depth bias
  differs; the acceptance target is “visually identical to the best Metal can do”,
  not numerical depth parity. Same-scene retail equivalence remains unverified.
- **Retina text:** crisp text requires the separately obtained FrancophilSans.ttf.
  Without it, the fallback scales retail glyphs. The font is not shipped here.
- **Better Trees mode 2:** Metal now uses the ruled shared values (strength 0.15,
  floor 0.15). The earlier Metal-only tuning (0.08 / 0.40, meant to reduce pale
  blue-grey haze from low-alpha twig texels promoted by distance fill) is gone. Whether
  that haze returns with the shared values has not been compared. See
  [pass reference](reference/macos_metal_passes.md).

## Vulkan backend

`src/render/vulkan/` (`docs/vulkan/PLAN.md`). Divergences from the DX11 backend that are left on
purpose or not yet closed, as of P6 (2026-10-25).

- **Mid-frame updates land early.** The upload batch (`resources.h`) is submitted before the frame
  that recorded it, so an `UpdateSubresource`-style update made between two draws of a frame lands
  before the whole frame. D3D11 orders it between the two draws. The engine updates its dynamic
  textures (minimap, UI atlases) between frames, so nothing visible is known; a mid-frame update
  would show one frame early.
- **MSAA resolve per main pass.** Retail renders the main target multisampled and resolves it into
  the back buffer once a frame (EndCommands). The Vulkan resolve-attachment path resolves at the end
  of every pass on the main target (several a frame: the frame copy and read-backs split it), and
  keeps the multisampled colour and depth stored rather than transient so the next pass can load
  them. The picture is the same; the cost is bandwidth. The tiler path
  (`VK_EXT_multisampled_render_to_single_sampled`, chosen when the device has it, the S25) resolves on
  the tile instead. It ran on the S25 in P6 (2026-10-25): the main menu rendered at 2x through it
  (`main targets 2340x1080, multisample 2x (tiler msrtss)`) before the phone defaults turned MSAA off.
  P7 measured it in game at half resolution: town GPU busy 32 -> 36 %, stress fps -9.6 %; the phone
  default stays off pending a ruling (`docs/android/P7_FIRST_PASS.md` C7).
- **Mixed sample counts.** A render buffer pairing the multisampled main colour with a single-sampled
  depth image (or the reverse) is rendered without the depth (logged once). D3D11 would refuse the
  draw. No route in the game builds one; it was only reached through the P0 attach bug fixed in P3.
- **Resolve outside a frame.** `RenderBuffer::Resolve` is recorded into the frame's command stream;
  one called outside BeginCommands / Finish is skipped (logged once). Every known caller
  (`FrameCopyImageBuffer::Generate` from the screenshot and the save thumbnail) runs inside a frame.
- **Read-back formats.** `ImageBufferData::Read` answers false for a compressed or depth image (the
  Metal backend's rule). Retail's staging copy is BGRA8 and D3D11 refuses those copies too, then reads
  the uninitialised staging texture; here the caller sees "not readable".
- **Validation residue (P2; narrowed in P7).** The byte-4 vertex attribute error is gone in P7: the
  reflection records each vertex input's numeric type (`kMappingVersion` 4) and an `int4` input fed by
  the engine's byte-4 element (`R8G8B8A8_UINT` on dx11, `vertexlayoutdata.cpp`) reads it as
  `R8G8B8A8_SINT` (`pipelines.cpp` AttributeFormat). That is the same value as D3D11's for every byte
  below 128; a byte of 128 or more would read negative here and positive on D3D11. No such byte has been
  seen and none has been ruled out (the element's data comes from the meshes, not checked). What stays:
  the shadow map's depth-only pass draws with pixel shaders that write `SV_Target` into no colour
  attachment (a validation warning, not an error), exactly as D3D11 binds a null render target. The
  Galaxy S25 run is validation-clean of errors since P7 (`docs/android/P7_FIRST_PASS.md` §A).
- **AO on the tiler MSAA path (P4): FIXED (P8 round 3, owner ruling).** On
  `VK_EXT_multisampled_render_to_single_sampled` (`caps.msrtss`) the main depth image is single-sampled:
  every main pass stores it through the implicit resolve (`SAMPLE_ZERO`), and the depth discard is off
  while AO asks for a readable depth. The AO pass reads that depth through ASSAO's non-MSAA resolve
  (`PSResolveDepth1x`); its apply runs in a main pass at the MSAA count. Verified on the S25 at MSAA 2x
  with AO on: validation clean of errors, the AO visible (`docs/android/P8_VULKAN.md` §7.1).
- **Update on a shared or BC4 image (P4).** `ImageBufferData::Update` answers false for an image that
  ext.share_gpu_data shares or ext.compress_ao made BC4 (the Metal backend's rule). D3D11 refuses the
  write to its IMMUTABLE texture without reporting it. No engine route updates a seeded texture.
- **Lazy objects re-made by the draw (P4).** With ext.create_on_demand, a draw makes again any bound
  buffer or texture that an idle eviction gave back since its Bind, under the table's use gate. D3D11
  keeps a reference on what is bound, so an eviction there frees nothing a draw still uses. Same
  picture; the re-make costs one materialization on that draw.
- **Linux runs on this backend, unverified on a device (P5).** Since P5 the Vulkan backend is the Linux
  renderer (the default configure off Windows and Apple) and the D3D11 route Linux used before is gone.
  The Linux build and the unit tests pass in CI (Ubuntu 24.04, the vulkan configure since P1); the four
  `tools/linux_run.sh` routes on lavapipe and the world comparison against the DX11 exe were NOT run in
  P5: WSL2 would not start (the hypervisor is off on the dev machine). No Linux-only divergence is known;
  none has been looked for on a running Linux exe yet. The real-GPU run is the owner's (checklist in
  `src/render/vulkan/README.md`).
- **Android: full-resolution rendering (P6) — FIXED in P7.** `VideoRenderScale` (phone first run 50)
  sizes the back buffer at half the window (1170x540 on the S25); the SDL layer scales the window size
  and pixel density by it so the mouse agrees, and the present pass scales the frame up (linear). Since
  P8 it is the Options > Enhanced row "Render scale" (Full / Half / Quarter), changed live
  (`docs/android/P8_INPUT_OPTIONS.md`).
- **Android: the UI grows with a smaller render scale (P8) — FIXED in P9.** The UI scale is now in
  screen units (`src/platform/common/ui_render_scale.h`, `docs/android/P9_LOADING_SCALE.md` §2): the
  interface keeps its size at Full, Half and Quarter. What P8 recorded: the UI was laid out in engine pixels and
  the render scale shrinks the engine's back buffer, so at User interface scale 100 the UI covers twice
  the screen share at Half (the phone's default since P7) and four times at Quarter: on the S25 at
  Quarter the Options dialog no longer fits (its tab row is above the screen; the Enhanced rows, Apply
  and Ok stay reachable, so Quarter can be undone). The player can lower User interface scale
  (`ext.phone_menu` lets it go to 25 %). Not changed: whether the UI scale should follow the render
  scale is a ruling.
- **Android: no in-game frame cap (P7) — FIXED in P8.** The game loop is now paced too
  (`FrameLimiter::TickInGame` from `GameUpdateLoop::Draw` and `ext.render_pipe`'s main half), to the
  settings cap `ext.fps_cap` (Options > Enhanced "Frame rate cap"; the phone's first run sets it on at
  60, ruled 2026-10-25) or `BANISHED_FPS_CAP`. Verified on the S25: 60.0 fps in game (was 120), live
  changes to 30 and Off.
- **Android: a captured mouse lost its buttons (P8, FIXED).** SDL's Java glue (pinned SDL 3.4.x) drops a
  captured pointer's `ACTION_MOVE` / `ACTION_BUTTON_PRESS` / `ACTION_BUTTON_RELEASE`: after a
  middle-button press put the window in relative mode (the camera-rotate drag), the release never reached
  SDL and the rotate stuck on. `OpenBASurface.java` passes them on; `docs/reference/android_mouse.md`.
  Unverified: a real mouse over the owner's KVM (the fix was checked with a uinput mouse). Round 2
  (owner's test): the slow rotate (only the last of a frame's batched samples was read) and the cursor
  snap after the release (Android shows the pointer where the capture began) FIXED; a jump to the screen's
  centre was not reproduced (a re-announced input device starts there): the owner's KVM to confirm.
- **Android: no shadows-off (P6).** Retail's shadow setting has three sizes and no off; the phone's
  first-run `settings.cfg` picks the smallest (`VideoShadowScalar=2`). A true off would be an extension.
- **Android: save on low memory or termination (P6; narrowed in P7).** Since P7 `SDL_EVENT_LOW_MEMORY`
  and `SDL_EVENT_TERMINATING` ask for a save (`app_android.cpp` lifecycle watch,
  `Platform::App::TakeSaveRequest`), which the next game update starts through the game's own autosave
  body (`AutoSaveDialog::Autosave`, with retail's refusals; retried every 120 frames, ten times).
  Verified on the S25: `am send-trim-memory org.openba.game RUNNING_CRITICAL` in a running town wrote
  `Auto.sav` the same second. What it does NOT cover: a LOW_MEMORY in the background (SDL sends one for
  every `onTrimMemory`, including the UI_HIDDEN of every trip home) is dropped, because the main thread
  is blocked there and nothing can save; a process Android kills in the background still loses what
  the last autosave did not hold. TERMINATING comes after SDL has queued the quit, so the quit usually
  wins. After a trip to the background the game's own pause dialog is up and the autosave body refuses
  (`UI PausesGame`), as retail's autosave timer does.
- **Android: the watchdog and the background (P6) — FIXED in P7.** The Android watchdog's hang budget
  is paused between WILL_ENTER_BACKGROUND and DID_ENTER_FOREGROUND (`boot_state_background.h`; the state
  file says `detail=background`). Verified on the S25: 40 s at the home screen read
  `RUNNING ... stale=0 detail=background`, and the frames ran on after the relaunch.
- **Android: run-time font rasteriser absent (P6).** `font_raster_posix.cpp` looks for the TTF beside
  `/proc/self/exe` (the system's `app_process64` on Android) and in the home and system font folders;
  none has it, so `ext.sharp_ui` keeps its retail fallback there.
- **Android: the pipeline cache persists on a clean quit only — FIXED in P7.** `vulkan_pipelines.bin`
  and the warm list are also written at WILL_ENTER_BACKGROUND (`PipelinesPersist`, from the mobile
  lifecycle watch in `device.cpp`). Verified on the S25: the file's mtime is the second of the HOME
  key press (`[Vulkan] pipeline cache: 355893 bytes written`). A kill while in the foreground (recents
  swipe of the running app, `am force-stop`) still keeps the file of the last background or quit.

## 15. `ResourceLimitUI` accepted a config's resource index only below 11 — FIXED

**Where.** `src/game/Dialog/resourcelimitui.cpp`, `resourcelimitui_sa::IdxFor`, used by
`ResourceLimitUI::OnCreated` / `OnUpdateLayout` / `OnElementEvent` (retail Slot4 / Slot7 / Slot9,
`0x140077970` / `0x1400778d0` / `0x1400777e0`).

**What the code did.** It used the bound config's resource index only when it was `< 11`, and otherwise
fell back to the dispatch order. The vanilla Town Hall shows only the 11 built-in limits, entries 0..10.
Retail's ledger has ten more, the custom limits `Custom0` .. `Custom9` (entries 11..20), and a mod that
adds rows for them names those entries: those rows read and wrote the Log, Stone, ... limits instead of
their own (changing the Custom0 limit changed the Log limit).

**What retail does.** Slot4 / 7 / 9 index the ledger (`CitizenBehavior::professionResources`, 21 entries
of 0x20 bytes) with `*(int*)(config + 0x20) * 0x20 + 8`, where `config` is `IsDerivedFrom(config) ? config :
null`, and no bound.

**Fix.** `IdxFor` returns the config's index directly and `ConfigOf` does retail's `IsDerivedFrom` check.
The dispatch-order fallback (`IdxForFallback` and its 11-entry registry) is removed:
`DialogInstance::AddController` (`src/engine/ui/dialog_create.cpp`) always stores the config before
`OnCreated`, so the premise behind the fallback (standalone never binds the config) no longer holds, and a
row with no config would crash retail too. Seen in a run before and after the change: the Custom rows are
separate from Log and survive a save and reload. No serialized state is touched; the resave gate was not
run by the author.
